A career guidance system that supports career selection processes that will allow children to discover their own abilities and recognise the professions that fit their abilities must be established immediately. It should be essential that universities become educational institutions that grow the manpower our country needs, produce science and technology by doing research, lead social development and compete with world universities. It is our youth's greatest expectation that education and employment in preparing for economic and social life are equal in all areas; that all institutions, rules and practices are shaped in line with the understanding of equality of opportunity. Young people's self-esteem and social belonging must be strengthened. While these steps are being taken, there are many differences among employees regarding administrative, financial and social rights as they do the same work in the same places and services, but their status is different. It is contrary to the principles of equality and justice guaranteed by the Constitution, and it has a negative effect on the motivation and the working peace of workers. In public, it must stop running unsecured, contractual, temporary, proxy, honorary, paid and part-time staff. During the stabilisation process, net exports contributed to growth historically to their highest levels, and for the first time since 2002, the 12-month savings of current transactions have increased due to significant improvement in external balance. The data on the last period indicates that the economic recovery continues. On the other hand, inflation has seen serious improvement since October of last year. As of November 2019, consumer inflation rose to more than 25 per cent in the past October due to the fluctuation of the exchange rate dropped to 10.6 per cent. In this rapid decline in inflation, it was effective that all macroeconomic policies were determined in a coordinated and focused way to combat inflation. The Central Bank raised policy interest in September last year in order to limit the negative effects of foreign currency development to its inflation outlook, and increased monetary policy predictability with the communication that firm monetary stance would be maintained until a significant improvement in inflation outlook was achieved. The policies put into place under the New Economic Programme to ensure sustainable growth and financial stability have played a key role in reducing disorientation and uncertainty in financial markets. In 2019, the stable course of Turkish lira, as well as the improvement in pricing behavior and inflation expectations, the moderate course in supply prices and import prices, were the main factors to support the decline in inflation. In addition, domestic demand conditions contributed to the decline in inflation. The ongoing recovery of inflation dynamics has allowed interest reductions in monetary policy starting in July 2019. The Central Bank has cut a total of 1,200 base points in policy interest due to significant improvement in inflation during the July-December period. Thus, policy interest was reduced from 24 per cent to 12 per cent. During this period, the low level of global economic activity navigation and global inflation supported the demand and risk appetite for developing country's financial assets, such as Turkey, by strengthening expectations that the developed central banks will continue their monetary policies in a broader way. In recent times, the country's risk premium has declined and the Turkish lira has continued its steady course, as policies followed in addition to support of global conditions improve in macroeconomic indicators. At this point, we predict that with the continuation of the strong policy co-ordination, inflation will continue to improve, and the annual inflation will be reduced to 12 percent, which is the target of YEP in 2019. At the end of 2020, after reaching its 8.5 per cent target set under the YEP again, 5 per cent will be approached in the middle term in line with price stability. In addition to increased interest reductions, the realisation of the credit channel supports Turkey's competitiveness in the global trade market and plays a key role in our economy's path towards a more balanced and sustainable growth path. The inequality in Turkey is not only limited to the inequality of income, but perhaps a more important problem is the inequality of wealth. Unfortunately, Turkey is a country where wealth inequality is very painful, but more importantly, in your power, this inequality is getting very bad. In the world today, wealth measures inequalities; who has a bank account, how much deposits, who holds stocks, who owns real estate; they're being looked at, they're being measured, they're being done all over the world. In our country, when you came to power, the richest 10 percent in 2002 had 67 percent of the wealth in Turkey, and today the richest 10 per cent have 82 per cent of the wealth in Turkey, so 90 per cent of the population in Turkey is content with 18 per cent of the wealth. In short, it's a very dramatic, very painful view, and on the other hand, it's getting much worse in your time. I also need to remind you that during budget talks, this has been brought up many times: the natural result of these inequalities is very common unemployment -- especially young unemployment -- is widespread poverty. And we've come to a very different and new situation in Turkey, a problem as painful as the unemployment issue, as much as the poor who worked in your time of power. And by the time we arrived from 2018 to 2019, it rose to over 4 million people over a year, over 4 million citizens working in Turkey today. With their families, we're talking about a much higher number, a much higher percentage. Another inequality. Opportunity inequality, my friends. It's a little bit more difficult to measure the inequality of opportunity, but I'm going to share data with you, and I think it's very important: who's doing education expenses in Turkey, who's spending how much money on their children? The richest 20 per cent make 63.6% of the education spending in Turkey, friends. The poorest 20 percent make 2.8 per cent of their education expenses. You know what that means? There are 23 stories of difference; for example, 20 percent of the richest people take 23 pencils to their child and send them to school, while 20 percent of the poorest people only take one pen and send it to school. 23 pencils on one side, 1 pencil on the other, 23 erasers on the other, 1 eraser on the other. In the 1930s, the Nuri Killigil facilities in Istanbul were also among the first private companies in the defence industry, and in 1940 the Nud-36 training aircraft was produced by the Nuri Demirag Plane Factory and the 6-member NUD-38 aircraft.
